ParlaMint-LV_2019-11-13-PT13-2018-U3	Let us start a debate. Name of Member Dana Reizniece-Ozola.
ParlaMint-LV_2019-11-13-PT13-2018-U4	Good morning, Madam President of the Saeima. Colleagues! Mr. Prime Minister! Ministers! On behalf of the opposition, I congratulate you today on the fact that we have already reached second reading in the budgetary process. The proposal is linked to the fight against the shadow economy. Where there is a large shadow economy, there is usually unfair competition, or distortion of competition and unfair business. And we, society as a whole, lose it. According to VARAM data, there are 105 thousand tons of tyres. That's a lot. At least in our own senses - even if we do not focus on the sector - we know clearly that this is a lot. We also see... We hear many stories about the stunned forests, about illegally accumulated tyres that are not recycled. Unfortunately, this is a reality in Latvia. Why? Because of extreme competition... due to competition in tyre management over the years, the cost of operators' services has fallen significantly... The cost of services has fallen, yes, while the natural resource tax has risen rapidly. In fact, management costs, if compared... During the 10-year period, things are usually growing, in which case they have fallen by three times. And at such cruel competition, tyre managers are starting to be dumping. And in essence, this leads to them not recycling tyres. Their actions give the impression that they're doing it, but in fact they're putting them up... and does not, because the actual costs for them are much higher than the actual costs of the service. As a general rule, when a tyre is sold, it must be accepted back at that point of sale or it must be clearly indicated where the tyre holder can do so. But what entrepreneurs often do - they sell tyres on the Internet without specifying where these tyres can be recycled, and thus avoid enforcement. This proposal relates precisely to the fact that, if tyres are sold on a website but the trader does not indicate where these tyres can be transferred for recycling, then the State Revenue Service, if they see that they are doing so in good faith, has the possibility to block this website (as in many other cases when the State Revenue Service sees that tax is being circumvented when a product or service is marketed in the internet environment and is being done in bad faith). This is a proposal that we submitted to you already in the budget package last year, together with other measures to put the tyre management system in order. And then the Minister of Environmental Protection and Regional Development promised that the budget... that without rushing this issue, the Ministry will come with its own solution over the course of the year. Unfortunately, almost a year has already passed, but there is no clear solution on the part of the Ministry of Environmental Protection and Regional Development, so we are re-submitting this proposal. It has no fiscal impact. This is a matter of putting the system right, which may also have an indirect positive impact later on the public budget revenue that we very much need at the moment. And we also call on us to support this proposal for our part.

However, I must understand that this proposal is intended to bring back this order... restore it as it was before. This is the distribution between this tax and the revenue from this tax between the State and the municipality. Let me explain why. Currently, gambling halls are located in various places - in cities, rural villages. Most of them are in the city centre. And there are very many cases where that municipality, the local community, those people have no real influence... And even when the municipal council voted 'no', then by court decision this gambling hall is still being set up, and this local community now has to deal with the problems that arise in nature and follow any gambling hall. It's both social and security, quite simply... what's happening around these gaming halls. Some funding for the municipal police, funding for the social service to fight these social clients! And if we are in favour of such an approach that local communities are taking out this money, then I think it would be very wrong if local communities, local authorities, residents who are housed with these gaming halls had no greater right to influence them to make them hard. There is no such possibility at the moment. Simply this gambling hall remains, but the revenue for them is removed. I think that is not really right. Therefore, I have already tabled proposals to extend this right to another law, which unfortunately is not part of the budget package. If money is taken away from the locals whose safety, social affairs, this money was mainly served, then we also give local communities the right to decide whether or not to their home, their doors have these gambling halls or not! This is a very serious proposal which, in my opinion, has not been debated. I know that the municipalities are against this proposal. I am also against this proposal if it does not come with the other one, which would mean that they too have the right to decide what is happening on their territory. At present, such a system does not exist. I therefore call for this proposal to be supported, to maintain this division. But if it is not supported, colleagues, be consistent and after we see another law... I'll remind you of another law. The proposal has already been tabled. I hope that Mr Bondara will look at this in the budget and financial (tax) commission in the near future. And then support another proposal that will, by its very nature, enable local authorities, local communities, to close these gambling halls quite simply hard so that they do not have to fight them. Because at the moment, there is some funding in the budget - practically very small, which is offered to municipalities... take care of security, about social matters... which is the result of this proposal. Thank you.

ParlaMint-LV_2019-11-13-PT13-2018-U122	This draft law provides that the amount of the old-age pension awarded may not be less than the amount of the minimum old-age pension, and that the Cabinet of Ministers shall determine the basis, amount, procedure for granting and reviewing the minimum old-age pension. ParlaMint-LV_2019-11-13-PT13-2018-U123	Members want a vote. I would also like to have a debate. Let us start a debate. Name of Member Dana Reizniece-Ozola.
ParlaMint-LV_2019-11-13-PT13-2018-U124	Colleagues! The principle of the national social insurance system is that you pay state social insurance contributions while working, are insured against various types of accidents or difficulties in life and you accumulate your old-age pension. That's the system. But where is the problem now? Despite the fact that very many people in Latvia have made this insurance and savings while living abroad, they cannot receive this old-age pension. There are, of course, exceptions. The European Union, the countries of the European Economic Area, are also certain countries with which we have treaties (Canada, Australia, Ukraine, Russia), but there are also very many countries with which we do not have these agreements, and then people cannot get an old-age pension. If you have returned to Latvia, you have a very difficult process to prove that you are practically living here... For example, the indication of the declared address alone is not enough. I can mention two countries with which we already have practical examples, where people have problems and where they could still be. One is the United States, where there are many of our citizens who have spent some time there and now want to live in Latvia. You can imagine that, especially at the beginning, it doesn't happen that you come right here and live all the time... for some time you live here, there for some time while you move, and experience shows that you have been refused to pay your old age pension on several occasions in such cases. The second example is Britain. Yes, it is now a country of the European Union, but soon it may not be a country of the European Union. We do not have a contract with this country in the field of social security. And taking into account the data from the Ministry of Foreign Affairs that we currently have almost 58 thousand Latvians living in Great Britain, it can become a real problem at some point. This is our proposal: if you have paid social insurance contributions, pension savings in Latvia, you can receive it, whether you live in or outside Latvia. I call for support.

ParlaMint-LV_2019-11-13-PT13-2018-U130	Colleagues! During the Kučinskis government and during the previous Saeima, the principle was introduced that, when reviewing the old-age pension, account is taken of insurance periods, or the number of years that a person has actually worked. The higher the length, the greater the indexation of the pension, applying a different percentage to the real increase in the amount of insurance contributions' salaries. Our proposal is to introduce another category in this graduation, with an index of 100 per cent if the person is 50 or more years old. Currently, the maximum threshold is 80 per cent for periods of 45 years or more. There's not a lot of such people, believe me. There is, for example, one gentleman living in Talsi who is over 80 years old. He's been working for 62 years. I think it would be such a respectful gesture from us as legislators that we would show that much work, also paying taxes, building up savings, investing in the development of Latvia's well-being is a matter of honour. This has no significant fiscal impact, with a small impact of - 0.9 per cent. But we can see that... For example, yesterday, the Ministry of Welfare redeployed several budget programmes, where the funds were left over. For example, disability pension alone... Countries that... which have more financial resources, can afford and also spend more on such things as social protection in general, social assistance. Of course, I will give examples. If we look at expenditure (as a percentage of gross domestic product) for social protection in the Nordic countries... These are the 2017 Eurostat data. In Denmark, 27.1 per cent of gross domestic product for social protection. Finland - 30.9 per cent of gross domestic product. In Sweden - 29.1 per cent of gross domestic product. In Norway - 28.4 per cent of gross domestic product. In Latvia - about 15 percent. So, colleagues, "to approach the Nordic countries" means - how? If your gross domestic product is increasing and you have more resources that you can afford to spend on social protection, then your spending on social protection - social benefits, child benefits, maintenance, and so on - is increasing. And previous governments, colleagues, introduced an automatic mechanism so that it would happen more or less automatically. As the government raises the minimum wage according to how wages in the economy grow, which, of course, is growing when gross domestic product increases, it ensures that this automatic mechanism is in place that a number of social expenditures grow in line with economic growth. That is exactly what the Nordic countries, colleagues, are doing. And what are you doing? You decouple it from the minimum wage. This is a promise that is absolutely the opposite of what the Nordic countries would do. Neither Finland, Sweden, Norway or Denmark would ever imagine doing something like that. Never! Colleagues! So I don't know if you understand at all what you're doing here with this. If not, think again. Thank you for your attention.

ParlaMint-LV_2019-11-13-PT13-2018-U286	Members want to have a debate. Name to Member Edgar Kucin.
ParlaMint-LV_2019-11-13-PT13-2018-U287	Hello, colleagues! Article 110 of the Satversme of the Republic of Latvia provides: “The State protects and supports marriage –... the family, the rights of parents and the rights of the child.” This means that the legislator envisages that the aid element may also include social functions which the State undertakes to perform. Currently, one of these social functions is free meals for pupils in grades 1-4. By providing funding for this, state-aid families whose children are studying in primary schools. Taking into account the current distribution of budget resources between state and local government and the proposed amendments regarding the financing of pupils' nutrition - 50 versus 50 (the state and local government respectively) - it can be concluded that the State waives its obligation to comply with its obligations under the regulatory enactment. I would like to draw your attention to the fact that the re-allocation of loads by special legal acts – proposed amendment, adjustment of both the Law on the Protection of Children and the Law on Education – by determining additional functions for municipalities according to Articles 7 and 8 of the Law “On Municipalities” is permitted only if the State provides additional funding to municipalities, determining new sources of income. In addition, upon the entry into force of these amendments, the State will receive additional... hidden... the source of funding, which... will have a negative impact on regional development as a whole. Thus, in order to comply with the norms of the legal act, which has the highest legal force, namely, the norms of the Satversme, amendments related to the percentage distribution of financial resources between the state and municipalities should also be made, increasing municipal funding. In the wording of the proposed amendments to the existing law there is a contradiction with the special legal norms in the law “On Municipalities” as well as with the constitutional norms of the Republic of Latvia. In particular, there is a precedent that gives municipalities the opportunity to bring an action before the Constitutional Court in order to defend their rights. The government has failed to respect the principle of legitimate expectations when drawing up its budget for 2020, i.e. it has ignored more than 10 laws... of the value included... Therefore, I call on the government to withdraw this draft budget and to present a new one in accordance with the laws previously adopted, otherwise such government actions are fundamentally undermining Latvia as a democratic and legal state. Thank you. (Applause)

ParlaMint-LV_2019-11-13-PT13-2018-U552	Dear colleagues, I would like to remind you of the 2016 Audit Statement of the State Audit Office, which was about whether the service of an assistant for a disabled person really provided... on the achievement of its purpose... The objective of the audit was to make sure that the assistant service was implemented in such a way as to achieve the objective of its creation: to ensure the integration of persons with disabilities into society. Statistics of the Ministry of Welfare show that the services of assistants in the... In 76 percent of cases, they are provided by family members or relatives. The audit found that in 59 percent of cases, the assistant is the closest member of the family - mother, father, spouse, or child. I would like to emphasise that in this audit data can be found on the number of beneficiaries of assistance services. The number of children receiving them is increasing every year. Key conclusions: The audit found that the activities of the Ministry of Welfare in developing the policy of equal opportunities of persons with disabilities were not sufficient to justify the assistance service as an instrument for achieving policy objectives. The need for a service has been established declaratively, without proper assessment of the actual needs of persons with disabilities, the services already available to them... the necessary changes, as well as without sufficient understanding of the objectives, content, way of providing them and the funding required for the implementation of the service. The objective of the service is to reduce the risk of the social exclusion of people with disabilities, and to promote the integration of people into society. Rather, it's... statements similar to those made in the light of slogans. However, other indicators to assess the effectiveness of the activities carried out by the Ministry of Welfare are not available in policy planning documents. At present, therefore, any activity which leads to the exit of a person with disabilities from his or her place of residence can be considered as a achievement of the objective pursued by the service. The goal – to relieve family members of persons with disabilities – is not achieved, as 76 percent of assistants are family members. After the crisis, the Ministry has not even taken advantage of the opportunity to improve the service by analysing possible scenarios for development and selecting the best type of service. The service is not transparent from 2013, when the full service was actually launched, or after the demand for the service needed to find additional means of providing the service. The most demanded type of assistant service - assistant service in municipality... is a step in the right direction. However, the Ministry of Welfare has not been able to establish an effective service administration system to ensure that the objective of the service is achieved and that the needs of people with disabilities are met. And a little bit about the experience of other European countries. Unlike our country, other European countries treat the services of an assistant much more widely, including the fact that the assistant provides not only a satellite but also support for other activities that a person is denied because of disability. For example, in Britain, a person with severe mobility problems who is moving on an electric wheelchair helps him to get up in the morning, to dress up, to break up, to go to work and to work, and to get the necessary activities, such as shopping, meeting friends, leisure trips, and others. There are differences in the amount of services, such as three hours per day for Spanish, up to 24 hours per day for France, but in some cases several assistants with a total number of hours not exceeding 32 hours per day may also be granted. For example, raising a person requiring the involvement of two persons. In Sweden, the municipality finances its own funds for the first 20 hours a week. If a person needs more services, it is financed by the State. If necessary, this can be achieved up to 48 hours a day, seven days a week. I even... I'm afraid to imagine what we're going to see in the next audit, so I ask you to support the proposal, both for Svekovas and for our... proposals. Thank you. ( Ploughs of opposition.)

If the unemployed becomes after termination of employment on the basis of the statement itself or because of an infringement, the unemployment benefit shall be granted from the date on which the application was lodged but not earlier than two months after the acquisition of the status of the unemployed person. In Latvia, the average contribution salary for unemployment benefit is calculated from the contribution salary for a period of 12 months, this period ending two calendar months before the month in which the unemployment status was obtained. In the case of a period of 30 years or more, the unemployment benefit is set at 65 per cent of the average contribution salary. This means that the country doesn't even give the unemployed, does not give them gifts, but takes 35 percent of their contributions for their administrative needs. ParlaMint-LV_2019-11-13-PT13-2018-U758	Okay, colleagues, let's get back to work! To the draft law “Amendments to the Law on Unemployment Insurance”. Proposal No 1. In fact, this proposal provides for a return to previous arrangements without reducing the length of unemployment benefits and reducing the amount of unemployment benefits. We also support this proposal for our part. Why? Here are a number of considerations. One. We had quite a serious discussion at the Budget and Finance (Tax) Commission about whether, without reducing the state social security contributions and at the same time reducing the service that the citizen receives for it, the Constitutional Regulations were not violated. And although the Ministry of Welfare considered that this was not done, I think there are significant risks here. In the Regulation of the Cabinet of Ministers No. 786 of the total rate - 35.09 per cent, which is currently paid as state social security contributions, very precisely determines how much of the percentage goes to a particular type of insurance. Part is health insurance, part - parental insurance, part - invalidity, state pension, and 1,84 per cent of social insurance is directed towards unemployment. Yes, it can be considered as a general principle of insurance. But if I asked as a private person how much of my social security contributions would go to unemployment, I would also be given the answer precisely that this amount of my social security contributions is intended for me in the case of unemployment. Consequently, I would ask you once again to reflect on this and to really understand that there are serious risks of conspiracy here. The other thing. That's a point. We also asked the Minister for Welfare what are the considerations why this support is being reduced. And those arguments are quite interesting. The Minister writes that the amendments are expected to motivate the unemployed to seek work more intensively and to return to the labour market more quickly. That can probably be understood, yes, because the aid will be much less. But the other part is to encourage employers to focus more on making better use of local labour supply. You tell me, please how this reduction in aid will motivate employers to use more local workers! If you, together with this proposal, were to reduce the amount of national social security contributions, that could be the case. The lower the tax and social contributions burden on the entrepreneur, the greater the motivation to take legal, local and not foreign workers and to employ them illegally. And the third. Talking about the farce. Mr Bondara was recently at one radio interview “Latvijas Radio” and seriously argued why it was necessary to rush the budget. And his argument was that it must be done now and quickly, because the situation is changing rapidly, the economy is going to get worse and we will be forced to reduce the forecasts for economic growth, and then the budget will have to be cut. Mr Bondara, you also position yourself as someone who understands from the economy. At a time when we are expecting a crisis like you are calling for, no reasonable politician is reducing unemployment benefits. ( The ZZS fraction is full.)

The share of people at risk of poverty appears to be declining but remains high, especially among vulnerable groups. In addition, the impact of social benefits on poverty reduction is relatively limited. ( Ploughs of opposition.)
